2. RECOGNITION 
 
A. TSD Mission Moment—Teachers’ College 
 
Tonight, we honor Barnard Elementary teacher Taryn Huang , who was 
accepted into the prestigious Teachers’ College at Columbia University 
August Writing Institute.  
 
The Writing Institute is designed for educators, classroom teachers, school 
administrators, and curriculum specialists who are committed to turning 
classrooms into richly literate reading and writing workshops. 
 
Applicants must apply and only a small number of applicants are a ccepted 
into the program each year. At this time, we would like to bring Taryn up to 
tell us a little more about what she hopes to learn and bring back to Troy. 
 
 
Recommended Action:  Present certificate after she speaks.

2. RECOGNITION 
 
 
B.  2016-2017 Outstanding Support Staff Award    
 
The Troy School District has many outstanding support staff employees. This evening 
we are proud to recognize the accomplishments of 16 individuals who were nominated 
for the Troy School District Outstanding Support Staff Award. 
The nominees represent exceptional staff at every level and job description. They were 
nominated by their colleagues , by parents and by former and current student s, all of 
whom talked about the impact these employees have on the lives of those around them. 
They exemplify our values and help us to ensure that when we say we ensure learning 
for all, ALL truly means ALL. 
We are grateful for their dedication and passion for our students. 
Would you please come forward as your name is called? (Distribute 
certificates/plants.) 
 
Rose Arnon, Custodian, Costello Elementary School 
Nahla Asmar, ELD Tutor, Boulan Park Middle School 
Margaret Baines, Facility Manager, Athens High School 
Dawn Copp, Health Care Aide, Martell Elementary School 
Janet Davert, Secretary, Baker Middle School 
Maxine Japowicz, Paraprofessional, Martell Elementary School 
Janie Kirchler, Health Care Aide, Hamilton Elementary School 
Sharon Kreza, Secretary, Wattles Elementary School 
Ivonne Mastracci, Secretary, Troy Union Elementary School 
Karyn Mroczka, Secretary, Smith Middle School 
Sue Ohlert, Secretary, Athens High School 
Joni Pallas, Paraprofessional, Special Education Larson Middle School 
Candy Parker, Bus Driver, TSD 
Erin Prendergast, Para/Resource Room, Troy Union Elementary School 
Betty Prospal, Secretary/Employee Services, TSD 
Seth Wyatt, paraeducator, Special Education, Costello Elementary School 
 
From this outstanding pool of nominees, Erin Prendergast was awarded the Troy School 
District Outstanding Support Staff Employee of the Year. Congratulations, Erin! 
Erin’s name was also sent to the county for consideration for the county -wide Betty 
Campion Outstanding Support Staff Award.  This is cer tainly an exciting and well -
deserved night for her. At this time, I would like to invite Erin Prendergast to say a few 
words.

2.       RECOGNITION 
 
D. AT&T Foundation Grant Award 
 
Earlier this fall, through the efforts of the Troy Community Foundation and 
Oakland County Commissioner Doug Tietz, the Troy School District received 
a $10,000 grant from the AT&T Foundation.   This grant has been used to 
provide transportation for high school students who participate in after 
school tutoring programs.   
 
Here with us this evening, is Oakland County Commissioner Doug Tietz and 
a representative from the AT&T Foundation to share a little more about this 
exciting grant.  
 
 
 
RECOMMENDED ACTION: Invite Doug Tietz up to say a few words  and introduce 
the AT&T representative.

2.       RECOGNITION 
 
E. Troy Foundation for Educational Excellence Mini-Grant Recipients 
 
This evening we are pleased to recognize the Troy Foundation for 
Educational Excellence and the work they do to support our schools, our 
students and our staff. This year, the Foundation funded 80 grants for a 
diverse group of projects to help enhance learning for students and enrich 
their educational opportunities.  
 
The Foundation is made up of volunteers who give of their time and talents 
to raise money  for these innovative projects that would not be possible 
without the resources the TFEE provides. 
 
At this time, I would like to introduce  Julie McClure, President of the Troy 
Foundation for Educational Excellence, who will briefly recognize the most 
recent group of grant recipients and the many ways they are enhancing the 
education of our Troy School District students. 
 
 
 
RECOMMENDED ACTION: Names of recipients/projects on screen as Mrs. McClure

                 2. F.     05.16.17 
 
 
2.       RECOGNITION 
 
 F.  National Teacher Appreciation Week 
 
Teacher Appreciation Week is observed nationally each year in May. This year, 
Teacher Appreciation Week was celebrated May 8th – May 12th.  At tonight’s 
meeting, the Troy School District and Board of Education would like to 
recognize all of our outstanding teachers by adopting the following resolution: 
 
 
WHEREAS, throughout Troy and across the country, teachers inspire, 
nurture and open children’s minds to new ideas,  
 
WHEREAS, teachers promote and strengthen the principles of democratic 
equality, develop leaders and encourage critical thinking; and 
 
WHEREAS, Troy teachers set the bar for excellence in education and 
continually exceed the highest standards in the profession; and 
 
WHEREAS, the goal of Teacher Appreciation Week is to raise national and 
local awareness of teaching excellence in building a better society; now,  
 
THEREFORE BE IT  RESOLVED, that the Troy School District supports 
National Teacher Appreciation Week, May 8-12, 2017, and encourages our 
schools and community to appropriately recognize the vital and changing role 
of teachers in imagining and creating a better future for society. 
 
 
RECOMMENDED ACTION:  Read and adopt resolution.

RECOGNITION  
TSD Mission Moment –  Smith 
Middle School “U -Knighted Hour”  
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Troy School District’s Outstanding 
Teachers 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
This past summer, a group of interested teache rs from 
Smith Middle School met to develop a “homeroom” for 
all Smith students.  As a building, Smith recognized the 
need to develop a stronger bond between staff and 
students and students between grade levels.  A plan was 
made to implement a 7 -period day 
once a month and 
have “U -Knighted” time during the 7 th hour.  Students 
were arranged by grade level and then divided amongst 
41 teachers resulting in approximately 17 students in 
each group. The committee meets before each U -
Knighted Hour and develops a le sson plan for the groups.  
The focus has been on character development, diversity, 
motivation, community, goal setting, and FUN!!!   This 
program has been well -received by both students and 
staff alike.  Assistant Principal  Amy Buchanan, Smith 
Middle Schoo l Counselor Jen Hartenburg, and social 
studies teacher Glen Rexer  were invited up to explain  
more about th eir program. They shared a brief 
PowerPoint presentation with the Board, which included 
testimonials from several Smith students.   
 
 
 
The Troy Scho ol District has many outstanding teachers 
on its staff. This evening we are proud to recognize the 
accomplishments of 38 individuals who were nominated 
for the Troy School District Outstanding Teacher Award 
Program. The nominees represent exemplary educators

It was moved by Mr. Schmidt  and supported by Mrs. 
Fleming that BE IT THEREFORE RESOLVED that the bid for 
classroom A/V displays be awarded to VSC in the amount 
of $718,743.85 and that this amount be approved as an 
expenditure of the 2016 Capital Projects Fund. 
  
Yes:           7 
No:            0 
 
Mr. Hauff aske d Mr. West to provide a brief explanation 
of the costs and how it will benefit students.  Mr. John 
Foster, consultant from Convergent Technologies, 
indicated that this is for display panels for secondary 
classrooms and this would be a closure of the gap fo r 
middle and high schools since they do not currently have 
the
m.  Mr. Hauff asked if they would be in place for fall.  
Mr. West confirmed that they would be.  
 
 
It was moved by Mrs. Hammond  and supported by Mr. 
Hauff that BE IT THEREFORE RESOLVED  that the bid for 
synchronized clock system be awarded to Sound 
Engineering in the amount of $406,473.00 and that this 
amount be approved as an expenditure of the 2016 
Capital Projects Fund. 
  
Yes:           7 
No:            0 
 
Mr. Schmidt asked Mr. West to share with the public why 
this equipment is needed.  Mr. West explained that it is a 
replacement of the current sync system for bells and is a 
result of last year’s project when the tube TV’s were 
removed from classrooms. There are no longer analog or 
digital clocks.

It was moved by Mrs. Fleming and supported by Mr. 
Miletti that BE IT THEREFORE RESOLVED  that the bid for 
district-wide container refuse collection services be 
awarded to GFL Environmental USA, Inc. based on their 
bid response and that this be approved as an expenditure 
of the 2017, 2018 and 2019 General Fund. 
 
Yes:           7 
No:            0 
 
Mr. Schmidt asked Mr. West to state for public viewers if 
this is consistent with what the District has been paying 
for current service.   Mr. West confirmed  that it was the 
low bid and is consistent with what we are paying now 
and will be for the next three years.

Maintenance Fund.

District.

nd annual Field Day and are planning a first -ever Prom

student council and will be held this Friday from 6 –  8:30 
p.m.  Honors Night is next Wednesday  and Prom for 
seniors is next week on Thursday.

School, shared that students will be taking AP tests in two 
weeks and that spr ing sports are in full swing.  N ext 
Tuesday is the enior Citizens Prom and is always a fun 
event.  The Science Olympiad team place d first in 
Regionals and DECA  students will be traveling f or a 
competition in Anaheim, California. 
 
Neha Paragi, senior at Athens was filling in for Ellory Thill 
as the student representative announced that teacher 
Adam Burns won the Inspirational Teacher Award and 
that teacher Kristy Pierce was a finalist fo r the MAHS 
Advisor of the Year award.    Neha also shared that spring 
sports are going on and that the Athens Ethnic Fair is 
being held on April 26th at 6 p.m.    
 
 
Dr. Machesky shared a brief Prezi with the Board 
providing an update  on where the District is in terms of 
the goal to become a world class district by 2020.   He 
reviewed the themes/goals that were pre sented to TSD 
staff at the beginning of each year since he became 
superintendent of the Troy School District in July 2014. 
53
Board of Education Meeting 7 April 18, 2017 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Board Comments 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
During his first year as superintendent the theme of 
“ oneTROY”  was introduced and  readily adopted.  The 
second year ’s theme was “ Take a Chance and Dare to 
Fail”  which created excitement and new opportunities for 
educators to dive deeper into instruction.  The 15-16 
school year focused on how we would do our work so the 
Teaching & Learning Department rolled out the core 
values of Deepening Learning, Focusing  Direction, and 
Creating a Collaborative Culture.  He talked about doing a 
few things better than anyone else even if it means 
saying no to some things.    During the 16-17 school year, 
the framework was set for our ambitious vision to be  a 
world class school by 2020.   
 
He also mentioned that the Board has been participating 
in book studies on world class learners  and how to 
connect learning to others by networking, and 
conferencing.   He talked about three guiding principles –  
systemness, one size does not fit all and cause vs. effect.   
 
 
Mr. Schmidt commented that those are bold and 
measurable goals and he is excited to keep going. 
 
Dr. Philippart talked about the District networking in the 
United States and outside  of the U.S., which  included a 
visit to  Canada to learn about other school systems and 
how it is a world benchmark.   
 
Mr. Hauff mentioned that the Parks and Recreation 
Advisory Board recognized 15 students from Wattles who 
were earning a governme nt badge and commended Dr. 
Brasington for the job she is doing in helping to develop 
the next generation of leaders. 
 
Mrs. Hammond announced that Teacher Appreciation 
week is next month and that she is personally grateful 
and humbled by the teacher s that she has interacted 
with and what amazing teachers we have in the District. 
 
Mr. Gottlieb echoed the same sentiments.  He also spoke 
about the ambiti ous outcomes the District has and the 
Teachers of the Year nomin ees.  He stated  he is honored 
to serve on the Board and is excited to see the District 
move forward.   
 
Mrs. Fleming congratulated all the teachers and stated

Intermediate School District budget and express either its support or 
disapproval of this budget.   
 
 
RECOMMENDED ACTION:  Adopt the following resolution. 
 
 
  Moved by  
 
 
  Supported by  
 
 
WHEREAS: 
 
 1. Section 624 of the Revised School Code, as amended, requires the intermediate 
school board to submit its proposed budget no later than May 1 of each year to 
the board of each constituent district for review; and 
 
 2. Not later than June 1 of each year, the board of each constituent district shall 
review the proposed intermediate school district budget, shall adopt a board 
resolution expressing its support for or disapproval of the proposed intermediate 
school district budget, and shall submit to the intermediate school board any 
specific objections and proposed changes the constituent district board has to 
the budget. 
 
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED THAT: 
 
 1. The Troy Board of Education has received and reviewed the proposed 
intermediate school district budget in accordance with Section 624 of the 
Revised School Code, as amended, and, by the adoption of this resolution, 
expresses its support for the proposed intermediate school district budget.  The 
Troy School District Board of Education believes that it is the responsibility of 
154
the Intermediate School District to be responsible for its own operations, 
including adoption of its budget.  The Troy School District’s s upport of the 
Oakland Intermediate School District’s budget is for the sole purpose of 
complying with Section 624 of the Revised School Code. 
 
 2. The secretary of the Troy Board of Education or his/her designee shall forward 
a copy of this resolution to the intermediate school board or its superintendent 
no later than June 1, 2017. 
 
 3. All resolutions insofar as they conflict with this resolution be and the same are 
hereby rescinded.

All capitalized terms herein shall have the same meanings as in the Lease.  Based upon the foregoing examination, and upon an examination of such other documents and matters of law as I have deemed necessary or appropriate, I am of the following opinions:  1. Lessee is a public body corporate and politic, duly organized and existing under the laws of the State of Michigan (the State), has one or more of the following sovereign powers: (a) the power to tax, (b) the power of eminent domain, or (c) police power, and is a political subdivision of the State within the meaning of Section 103(c) of the Internal Revenue Code of 1986, as amended, through the date hereof.  2. Lessee has the requisite power and authority to purchase the Equipment and to execute and deliver the Lease and to perform its obligations under the Lease.  3. The Lease has been duly authorized, executed and delivered by Lessee and, assuming due authorization, execution and delivery thereof by Lessor, constitutes the legal, valid and binding obligation of Lessee, enforceable against Lessee in accordance with its terms, and do not exceed or violate any usury law or any statutory or case law prohibiting or restricting the incurring of debt by Lessee; except that the enforcement of the Lease may be subject to any applicable bankruptcy, insolvency, moratorium or other laws or equitable principles affecting the enforcement of creditors rights generally.  4. Pursuant to the provisions of Michigan Act 99 of 1933, as amended, Lessee is obligated to include in its budget annual a sum sufficient to meet its obligations under the Lease and must levy sufficient taxes to provide that sum, subject to applicable constitutional and statutory tax rate limitations.   5. The Equipment leased pursuant to the Lease constitutes personal property and when subjected to use by Lessee will not be or become a fixture under applicable law.  6. The authorization, approval and execution of the Lease and all other proceedings of Lessee and Lessees governing body relating to the transactions contemplated thereby have been performed in accordance with all open meeting laws, public bidding laws and all other applicable state and federal laws.  7. There is no proceeding pending or threatened in any court or before any governmental authority or arbitration board or tribunal that, if adversely determined, would adversely affect the transactions contemplated by the Lease or the security interest of Lessor or its assigns, as the case may be, in the Equipment.  8. The entering into and performance under the Lease by the Lessee does not violate any judgment, order, law or regulation applicable to Lessee or result in any breach of, or constitute a default under, or result in the creation of any lien, charge, security interest or other encumbrance upon any assets of Lessee or on the Equipment pursuant to any indenture, mortgage, deed of trust, bank loan or credit lease or other instrument to which Lessee is a party or by which it or its assets may be bound, except as provided in the Lease. Section 5000.03 - Purchasing 
 
The Board authorizes the Superintendent to purchase and/or supervise the purchase of all materials, 
equipment, supplies and services necessary for the operation of the School District.  It is the intent of the 
Board that the School District purchase competitively, without prejudice or favoritism, and seek maximum 
value for all expenditures.  Competitive pricing is to be pursued by following legally required processes, as 
well as through such methods as cooperative purchasing, formal or informal bidding, requests for quotations 
from multiple vendors, and negotiation. 
 
Competitive Bidding and Micro-Purchases   The Board seeks to ensure the Dis trict complies with all 
competitive bidding and micro -purchase guidelines contained in the Education Department General 
Administrative Regulations (EDGAR).  Specifically, that “all procurement transactions must be conducted 
in a manner providing full and o pen competition consistent with the standards of this section.”  2 CFR § 
200.319.  The Board further acknowledges that these requirements apply specifically to micro -purchases, 
which are defined by EDGAR as: “a purchase of supplies or services using simpli fied acquisition 
procedures, the aggregate amount of which does not exceed the micro -purchase threshold.”  2 CFR § 
200.67.  The Superintendent will delegate responsibility for the development of procedures that comply with 
this policy to the Assistant Superintendent for Business Services. 
 
Automated Clearing House (ACH) Arrangements and Electronic Transaction of Funds   This Board 
has determined that it is in the best interest of the District to make certain District financial transactions by 
electronic payments.  Accordingly, the Board has adopted a resolution authorizing the School District to 
utilize electronic transactions.  It is contemplated that the District shall be a party to an Automated Clearing 
House (ACH) arrangement.  The Superintendent shall be responsible for the District's ACH agreements, 
including payment approval, accounting, reporting, and generally for overseeing compliance with the ACH 
policy.
